<note>This folio contains a portion of a mathematical log table from the *Shuli Jingyun* (數理精蘊, "Collected Essences of Mathematical Principles"), an encyclopedic work on mathematics commissioned by the Kangxi Emperor and published in 1723. The large characters in each cell represent the number, and the smaller characters represent the decimal part of its common logarithm.</note>

# 欽定四庫全書
## 卷四之五

<header>御製數理精蘊表</header>

<warning>Small interlinear glosses are extremely fine and occasionally blurred or faded, making some digits difficult to distinguish with certainty. Transcriptions of glosses represent best-effort readings of the characters' shapes based on the mathematical patterns of the Shuli Jingyun (数理精蕴) tables.</warning>

<meta>Mathematical or astronomical table, likely from a Jesuit-influenced Chinese scientific work like the 'Chongzhen Lishu' (崇禎曆書) or 'Shuli Jingyun' (數理精蘊). The large numbers in each cell decrease from left to right and increase downwards. The smaller interlinear text (gloss) likely represents fractions, differences, or higher-precision decimal places.</meta>
<warning>The interlinear glosses are extremely small and densely packed, leading to potential minor inaccuracies in the transcription of the smallest digits.</warning>
